in})\rangle\mathchoice{Q^{{{D^{\prime\prime}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.45055pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.45055pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32457pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.21039pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.94875pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.94875pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.61789pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.4199pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{E^{\prime\prime}}}}}{Q^{{{D^{\prime\prime}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.45055pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.45055pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32457pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.21039pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.94875pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.94875pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.61789pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.4199pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{E^{\prime\prime}}}}}{Q^{{{D^{\prime\prime}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.45055pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.45055pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32457pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.21039pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.94875pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.94875pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.61789pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.4199pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{E^{\prime\prime}}}}}{Q^{{{D^{\prime\prime}}\mathchoice{\makebox[9.45055pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.45055pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.32457pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.21039pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.94875pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.94875pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.61789pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.4199pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{E^{\prime\prime}}}}}_{h[\mathcal{C}_{\text{\it in}}^{+}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\text{\it in}}^{-}]\xi}[\mathcal{C}_{\text{\it in}}^{-}]{T}^{\dagger}_{D^{\prime\prime}}=0.

(54)

In the same way, it is easy to show that the amplitudes realise local charge conservation at the corner, where the future and past states intersect, i.e.

\boldsymbol{A}\big(Q_{\xi}[\mathcal{C}_{\text{\it in}}^{+}]\gamma(\text{\it in})\rightarrow\gamma(\text{\it out})\big)=\boldsymbol{A}\big(\gamma(\text{\it in})\rightarrow Q_{h[\mathcal{C}_{\text{\it in}}^{+}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\text{\it out}}^{+}]\xi}[\mathcal{C}_{\text{\it out}}^{+}]\gamma(\text{\it out})\big).

(55)

5
Summary and discussion

To summarize, we introduced amplitudes (
47
) that satisfy Ward identities (
52
,
51
) and charge conservation laws (
55
) across the two-dimensional corner
\mathcal{C}_{\text{\it out}}^{+}
, where the
in
-states and
out
-states intersect, see
Figure 4
for an illustration. The charge conservation laws are reminiscent of recent developments in perturbative gravity, where the gravitational
S
-matrix satisfies a tower of soft-graviton theorems, which have been generalized formally to all orders in the perturbative
r^{-1}
expansion in the vicinity of asymptotic future (past) null infinity
[
104
,
105
,
106
]
. These conservation laws follow from gravitational Ward identities
11
11

11

They are obtained from the pull-back of the Wheeler DeWitt equations to future (past) null infinity
\mathcal{I}_{\pm}
.
and impose pointwise matching conditions between local charge densities (energy, momentum and higher charge multipoles) at the asymptotic two-sphere where the future and past components of the asymptotic null boundary intersect. It is however unclear at this point whether these symmetries are stringent enough to determine the
S
-matrix elements completely. In here, we took a top-down perspective instead. Rather than starting from a presupposed
S
-matrix, which may not exist in a full theory of quantum gravity, we merely asked how local amplitudes for the quantum gravitational field could be constructed from the underlying symmetries alone. The key building block is the generalized projector (
35
) onto physical states. This projector defines an intertwiner for the gauge symmetry group at the null boundary. If we then also assume that there are certain preferred bulk and corner vacua
|\mathchoice{\Omega^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1375pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A^{\prime}}}}}{\Omega^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1375pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A^{\prime}}}}}{\Omega^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1375pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A^{\prime}}}}}{\Omega^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1375pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A^{\prime}}}}}\rangle
and
T^{A}
that can act as intertwiners
12
12

12

That the vacuum states
|\mathchoice{\Omega^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1375pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A^{\prime}}}}}{\Omega^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1375pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A^{\prime}}}}}{\Omega^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1375pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A^{\prime}}}}}{\Omega^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1375pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A^{\prime}}}}}\rangle
at
\mathcal{N}
and
T^{A}
at
\mathcal{C}_{\pm}
transform covariantly under the boundary symmetries can be understood as a realisation of the
quantum equivalence principle
recently introduced
[
107
,
108
]
.
for the corner symmetry algebra, (
44
), (
45
) and (
46
), we saw that it is possible to construct amplitudes from simple tensor contractions of the vacuum states and generalized projectors alone. The underlying pattern of index contractions is inferred from the topological gluing between the various components of the null boundary (see
Figure 4
).

Throughout our discussion, we were deliberately vague about how to realise the proposal in practice. The key building block is the projector (
35
) that maps kinematical boundary states to physical states on an abstract null boundary. Let us now provide some background material how this formal structure could be realized in practice. First of all, we shall note that the same formal structure also appears in the spinfoam and group field theory (GFTs) approaches to quantum gravity,
[
5
,
109
,
101
,
102
,
110
,
111
,
112
,
113
,
114
,
115
,
116
,
117
,
118
,
119
]
and
[
120
,
121
,
122
,
123
]
, in which the transition amplitudes are matrix elements of a generalized projector on a kinematical boundary Hilbert space. Spinfoams realise the projector through a sum over surfaces path integral between the respective
in
-states and
out
-states. GFTs define the projector through an auxiliary quantum field theory on the Wheelerian superspace of a single atom of space. Recent results on the quantum geometry of the null cone suggest a slightly different strategy to realise the same goal. The idea is to define the projector through the
n
-point functions of an auxiliary conformal field theory
[
19
,
11
]
. Schematically,

0}=\alpha_{i}}^{\phi_{i}|_{r\rightarrow\infty}=\bar{\beta}_{i}}\mathcal{D}\phi_{i}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\int d^{2}z\,\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{CFT}}[\phi_{i},\mathrm{d}\phi_{i}]}.

(56)

In here, we implicitly assumed a state operator correspondence between the gravitational boundary states
|\mathchoice{\alpha^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1375pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A^{\prime}}}}}{\alpha^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1375pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A^{\prime}}}}}{\alpha^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1375pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A^{\prime}}}}}{\alpha^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1375pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A^{\prime}}}}}\rangle,|\mathchoice{\beta^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.1375pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.0875pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{A^{\prime}}}}}{\beta^{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.8614pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.8614pt][c and the field configurations
\alpha_{i},\beta_{i},\dots
of the CFT. A proposal for how to construct this CFT compatible with earlier developments in loop quantum gravity
[
48
,
49
,
50
,
51
]
was put forward recently in
[
11
]
. This proposal is complementary to our present investigation. While we dealt here in this paper with a top-down approach to the fundamental amplitudes, the earlier proposal is based on a more standard bottom-up canonical quantisation of the gravitational phase space on a light-like boundary. The present framework, on the other hand, is more abstract. It does not rely on a concrete Hilbert space realisation and can serve as a blueprint for local amplitudes in various approaches.
